AGV B carries parts from line-side storage to the fixture workstations, from testing to the temperature chamber, from laser marking to finished-goods storage, and from finished-goods storage to the outbound buffer.\n\nOperators handle receiving to the inbound buffer, the outbound buffer to dispatch, fixture workstations to the loading table, the unloading table to the test workstations, and the temperature chamber to laser marking. They also load and unload the AGVs and conveyor. The conveyor carries containers between the loading and unloading ends of its cell. Handoffs at process machines are external manual transfers; automatic machine docking is not required.\n\nOperators perform these daily tool-service round trips:\n- Tool service for CNC A, 2 trips per day: tool cabinet A -> CNC A -> tool cabinet A.\n- Tool service for vertical machining center A, 2 trips per day: tool cabinet A -> vertical machining center A -> tool cabinet A.\n- Tool service for CNC B, 2 trips per day: tool cabinet B -> CNC B -> tool cabinet B.\n- Tool service for vertical machining center B, 2 trips per day: tool cabinet B -> vertical machining center B -> tool cabinet B.\n\nEVALUATION ASSUMPTIONS\n\nAGV transport cost is an index based on usable travel paths: loaded travel costs 1 index unit per meter and empty travel costs 0.8. Use one batch per trip. After each delivery, the empty AGV returns to its pickup point. Group shuttle jobs by pickup location in their first occurrence in the product routes. Include empty travel between groups and the daily departure from and return to the parking bay. These are planning assumptions, not measured charges, energy use, or a fleet scheduling simulation.\n\nRelocation effort is measured from the accepted Generate positions. A moved object incurs a fixed effort plus its horizontal Manhattan displacement multiplied by a per-meter coefficient. Rotation alone has no charge. For an AGV, this refers to moving its parking bay. The following fixed and per-meter coefficients are planning indices, not actual moving fees:\n- CNC machining center: fixed effort 8; distance effort 0.6 per meter.\n- Vertical machining center: fixed effort 10; distance effort 0.8 per meter.\n- Turn-mill machining center: fixed effort 16; distance effort 1.2 per meter.\n- Radial drilling machine: fixed effort 5; distance effort 0.4 per meter.\n- EDM drilling machine: fixed effort 6; distance effort 0.5 per meter.\n- Fixture workstation: fixed effort 3; distance effort 0.2 per meter.\n- Flexible test workstation: fixed effort 4; distance effort 0.25 per meter.\n- Laser marking station: fixed effort 3; distance effort 0.2 per meter.\n- Temperature chamber: fixed effort 6; distance effort 0.4 per meter.\n- Tool cabinet: fixed effort 1; distance effort 0.1 per meter.\n- Work table: fixed effort 1; distance effort 0.08 per meter.\n- Storage rack: fixed effort 2; distance effort 0.1 per meter.\n- Line-side storage: fixed effort 2; distance effort 0.1 per meter.\n- Conveyor: fixed effort 8; distance effort 0.35 per meter.\n- AGV: fixed effort 0.5; distance effort 0.02 per meter.\n\nWhen optimization is requested, give AGV transport cost and relocation effort equal importance. A moved object incurs a fixed effort plus its horizontal Manhattan displacement multiplied by a per-meter coefficient. Rotation alone has no charge. For an AGV, this refers to moving its parking bay.
